# **ChainTorrent MVP**

## Problem Statement

JavaScript dependency distribution runs through a single centralized registry that is an availability chokepoint, a mutable source of truth, and an intermediary between the people who publish packages and the people who consume them. ChainTorrent's MVP replaces that path with a content-addressed swarm, a canonical on-chain identity registry, and transferable access entitlements, so that the distribution and identity model is proven with monetization out of bounds.

**Adoption runs individual developer first, and everything else follows from that.** A developer at a terminal gains cross-project reuse of whatever their machine has already fetched, swarm retrieval on popular packages, and installs that survive a registry outage. Build platforms adopt next and for their own reasons: a service running the same few thousand popular installs continuously, on always-on machines with real bandwidth, holds the ideal cache as a by-product of its own economics, and seeding that cache costs almost nothing once it exists. That makes them natural superseeders rather than participants anyone has to recruit, and it is why no privileged bootstrap host is part of this plan. CI and enterprise adoption arrive as a consequence of that sequence rather than as targets to be won early.

The sequence matters to this workplan because it determines what the MVP measures. Latency is judged against interactive local installs rather than pipeline tolerance, since a developer notices a doubled install where a pipeline does not. Cache reuse is the headline adoption metric, because it is the benefit the adopting population actually experiences. Registry-outage survival remains true throughout and is simply not the lead, since it matters most to the population that adopts last.

The design is specified in `docs/research/cryptography.md` and bounded in `docs/research/MVP Scope.md`. Undetermined decisions are held in the To-Do list below.

The cryptographic construction is specified; its research record is `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`, and its statement is `docs/research/cryptography-critical-path.md`. A validation harness on both pairing curves produces the sizes, timings, and gas against the configured piece-group size and curve defaults, and nothing waits on it: every node reads the piece-group size from the deployment record and depends only on the code it calls.

## Objectives

Deliver an install path for JavaScript dependencies that survives registry outages, serves popular packages from a peer swarm, and exercises the identity and entitlement pipeline end to end.

Prove distribution and identity. Willingness to pay and seeder compensation are out of bounds and are held in the To-Do list; the transaction flow is proven at a nominal price on assets the project publishes, as a test instrument rather than a monetization step.

Every undetermined decision is carried in the To-Do list, which every workplan that stems from this one inherits.

## Expected Outcome

A developer installs dependencies and receives a working `node_modules` assembled from a local content-addressed store, a peer swarm, or the ingest source as fallback, with canonical identity resolved on-chain and an access entitlement minted to their wallet.

* `[ ]`   `harness-crypto/vectors/cross_holder` **The cross-holder agreement vector set, generic over the credential KEM its caller hands it: per record a parameter set set up under the admitted identity's setup scope, the admitted identity's element, a credential issued and its rerandomized successor, a capsule encapsulated under the set with the value it encapsulates, the value each credential decapsulates, and the uniforms it drew; owns the record type; creates the `vectors` module**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: every valid credential recovers the same value from every well-formed capsule, and a rerandomized credential recovers what the credential it was made from recovers, so the harness holds, for each record, an issued credential and its successor under one parameter set beside one capsule and the value it encapsulates, with each credential's decapsulation and the uniforms the record drew, as the algebraic cross-holder vectors (CR-08 cross-holder agreement; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/vectors/cross_holder` row and its conflict flag on the vectors as a composing function over functions it owns; the Credential KEM milestone's exit, cross-holder agreement on the encapsulated value; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope, `harness-crypto/vectors` composed over `harness-crypto/vectors/cross_holder`)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the identity arrives admitted as an `AdmittedKemIdentity`, its `setup_scope` the scope every record's setup takes and its `identity` the identity every record's derivation takes; the function admits no identity and refuses none
    * `[ ]`   Functional: each record sets up a parameter set from three uniforms under the admitted identity's setup scope, derives the admitted identity's element, issues a credential from a fourth uniform, rerandomizes it into its successor from a fifth, encapsulates a capsule from a sixth, and decapsulates the capsule with the issued credential and with the successor
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every uniform has the length the KEM's pairing's scalar sampling bound declares, and a record's draws are its uniforms concatenated in draw order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the function returns the configured count of records in index order
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a failed draw, setup, derivation, issuance, rerandomization, or encapsulation is each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, scope, count, or identity; the count arrives in its params and the admitted identity in its payload

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: a rerandomized credential is valid for the identity the credential it was made from is valid for, and rerandomization needs no master scalar, so the harness holds, for each record, a parameter set, the admitted identity's element, an issued credential and its rerandomization, each credential's validity verdict under the record's parameter set and identity element, and the uniforms the record drew, as the algebraic rerandomized-validity vectors (CR-08 rerandomized-credential validity and rerandomization requiring no master scalar; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/vectors/rerandomization` row and its conflict flag on the vectors as a composing function over functions it owns; the Credential KEM milestone's exit, rerandomized-credential validity; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope, `harness-crypto/vectors` composed over `harness-crypto/vectors/rerandomization`)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the identity arrives admitted as an `AdmittedKemIdentity`, its `setup_scope` the scope every record's setup takes and its `identity` the identity every record's derivation takes; the function admits no identity and refuses none
    * `[ ]`   Functional: each record sets up a parameter set from three uniforms under the admitted identity's setup scope, derives the admitted identity's element, issues a credential from a fourth uniform, rerandomizes it from a fifth with no master scalar, and checks each of the two credentials with `is_valid` under the record's parameter set and identity element
    * `[✅]`   Functional: every uniform has the length the KEM's pairing's scalar sampling bound declares, and a record's draws are its uniforms concatenated in draw order
    * `[✅]`   Functional: the function returns the configured count of records in index order
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a failed draw, setup, derivation, issuance, or rerandomization is each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, scope, count, or identity; the count arrives in its params and the admitted identity in its payload

    * `[ ]`   Functional: when the admitted identity's `setup_scope` is `SetupScope::Asset`, the function returns an empty set, making no draw and calling no KEM method

    * `[✅]`   Functional: a failed draw, setup, derivation, or issuance is returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, count, or identity; the count arrives in its params and the two admitted identities in its payload

* `[ ]`   `harness-crypto/vectors/cross_set` **The cross-set agreement vector set, generic over the credential KEM and the encoder its caller hands it: per record a piece-group key drawn once, two parameter sets set up independently under the admitted identity's setup scope, and for each set the admitted identity's element, an issued credential, a capsule with the value it encapsulates, the drawn key wrapped under that value over the configured derivation context, the value the credential decapsulates, and the key unwrapped with it; and the uniforms it drew; owns the record type**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: the encryptor chooses each piece-group key independently of any parameter set and every live set's sidecar entry carries that key wrapped under the set's own wrapping key, so two independently generated sets must each open the one key, which is what lets one ciphertext decrypt under every live set; the harness holds, for each record, one drawn piece-group key, two parameter sets set up from independent draws, each set's wrap of that key under its encapsulated value over the configured derivation context and its unwrap with the value a credential under that set decapsulates, and the uniforms the record drew, as the algebraic cross-set vectors (CR-08 cross-set agreement; CR-11 wrap and unwrap; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/vectors/cross_set` row and its conflict flags on the vectors as a composing function over functions it owns and on the wrap preceding the cipher; the Credential KEM milestone's exit, two independently generated parameter sets unwrapping one piece-group key through the wrap; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope, `harness-crypto/vectors` composed over `harness-crypto/vectors/cross_set`; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Credential KEM statement)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the identity arrives admitted as an `AdmittedKemIdentity`, its `setup_scope` the scope every set's setup takes and its `identity` the identity every set's derivation takes; the function admits no identity and refuses none
    * `[ ]`   Functional: each record draws the piece-group key once, at the piece-group key's length, and admits it as a `PieceGroupKey`
    * `[ ]`   Functional: each record sets up its first and then its second parameter set, each from three uniforms of its own under the admitted identity's setup scope, derives the admitted identity's element under the set, issues a credential from a fourth uniform, encapsulates a capsule from a fifth, wraps the drawn key under the encapsulated value over the configured derivation context, decapsulates the capsule with the issued credential, and unwraps the wrapped key with the decapsulated value over the same context
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the piece-group key's draw has the piece-group key's length, every other uniform has the length the KEM's pairing's scalar sampling bound declares, and a record's draws are its uniforms concatenated in draw order
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the function returns the configured count of records in index order
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a failed draw, piece-group key admission, setup, derivation, issuance, encapsulation, wrap, or unwrap is each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, scope, count, identity, or context; the count arrives in its params and the admitted identity and the derivation context in its payload

  * `[ ]`   `role`
    * `[ ]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the harness's vectors own, beneath the `vectors` module of `apps/harness-crypto`
    * `[ ]`   Owns `CrossSetVector`, `CrossSetMember`, and the function's deps, params, payload, success, error, return, and signature types; consumes `VectorCount`, which `harness-crypto/generate/evm/group_vectors` owns, and `PieceGroupKey`, `WrappedPieceGroupKey`, and the wrap's and unwrap's signatures, which `workflows/sidecar/wrap` and `workflows/sidecar/unwrap` own

    * `[ ]`   Computes through the KEM, the key-derivation adapter, and the encoder it is handed, and through `wrap_piece_group_key` and `unwrap_piece_group_key`; does not set up, issue, encapsulate, decapsulate, derive, encode, wrap, or unwrap itself
    * `[ ]`   Does not choose a count, an identity, or a derivation context, and does not admit an identity; the configuration carries them, the KEM factory admits the scope, `harness-crypto/config/admit_statements` admits the identity, `harness-crypto/run/kem_consumer` pairs it with its setup scope as an `AdmittedKemIdentity`, and the caller passes them here

    * `[ ]`   Does not carry a commit

* `[ ]`   `harness-crypto/vectors/statement_mutations` **The statement-mutation vector set, generic over the selected pairing, the credential KEM, the key agreement, and the delivery proof with its chain forms: per record one mint delivery from `harness-crypto/delivery/mint` over the typed mint context and the admitted identity and the transfer delivery from `harness-crypto/delivery/transfer` that follows it over the typed transfer context and the mint delivery's recipient, each baseline statement verified against its proof, and for every statement field and every proof scalar of each relation that field or scalar mutated alone with the mutated statement, the proof, and the reference verifier's verdict; and the deliveries' uniforms followed by its own mutation-offset uniform; composes no delivery step and admits no context or identity; owns the record, mutation, and field types**

  * `[ ]`   `objective`
    * `[ ]`   Problem: a delivery proof is valid for exactly one settlement, so every field of the statement it proves and every scalar of the proof must be bound, and the Rust reference verifier must refuse a statement differing in any one field and a proof with any one scalar changed, in both verifier forms; the harness holds, for each record, a mint statement and a transfer statement with their proofs, each field and each proof scalar mutated alone, each mutation with the reference verifier's verdict, the mutated statement, and the proof it was verified against, and the uniforms the record drew, as the algebraic mutation vectors (CR-09 mutated statement fields and responses; CD-03; the dependency map's `harness-crypto/vectors/statement_mutations` row and its conflict flag on the vectors as a composing function over functions it owns; the Delivery proof and on-chain verifier milestone's scope, `harness-crypto/vectors` composed over `harness-crypto/vectors/statement_mutations`, and its exit, CR-09 in both verifier forms with every statement field and response mutated; `docs/research/cryptography.md`'s Delivery Proof statement, the challenge hashing the full statement; `docs/research/cryptography-research-notebook.md`'s claim (v), non-malleability)
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the contexts arrive typed, in the delivery proof's forms, each carrying its purpose, and the identity arrives admitted, paired with its setup scope; the function rebuilds no form of a baseline context and admits no identity
    * `[ ]`   Functional: each record is one mint delivery over the mint context and the admitted identity and the transfer delivery that follows it over the transfer context, the mint delivery's parameter set and identity element, and its recipient as the holder; the record's baseline mint statement and proof are the mint delivery's own and its baseline transfer statement and proof the transfer delivery's own
    * `[ ]`   Functional: each record verifies each baseline statement against its proof, then draws its own uniform and samples it as the mutation offset
    * `[ ]`   Functional: for each field of each relation's statement in transcript order, then each scalar of its proof in witness order, the record holds a mutation: the baseline statement with that field alone replaced and the baseline proof, or the baseline statement and the baseline proof with that scalar alone replaced, and the reference verifier's verdict over the pair
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a first-group point is replaced by its sum with the first-group generator multiplied by the mutation offset, a second-group point by its sum with the second-group generator multiplied by the offset, and a proof scalar by its sum with the offset; a form-typed field by the form its `from_field` rebuilds from its canonical field value with the lowest bit of its last byte flipped, or, for an integer kind, its lowest bit flipped; the suite identifier by the identifier with the lowest bit of its last byte flipped and its version unchanged; the asset identity hash and the parameter-set identifier by their bytes with the lowest bit of the last byte flipped; the purpose by the other purpose of its relation; and the expiry by the expiry with its lowest bit flipped
    * `[ ]`   Functional: every uniform has the length the pairing's scalar sampling bound declares; a record's draws are the mint delivery's six uniforms in the order master, `u0`, `u1`, issue, recipient `x`, and recipient `y`, then the transfer delivery's three, buyer `x`, buyer `y`, and the rerandomization, then the record's own mutation-offset uniform, concatenated; the envelope coins and the proof nonces are drawn inside their adapters and are not recorded
    * `[ ]`   Functional: the function returns the configured count of records in index order
    * `[ ]`   Functional: a failed mint delivery and a failed transfer delivery are each returned unchanged in the error arm, the delivery's own error whole; a refused mutated form, suite identifier, asset identity hash, or parameter-set identifier, and a failed offset draw, verification, offset sampling, or proof rebuild are each returned unchanged in the error arm
    * `[ ]`   Non-functional: the function reads no clock and touches no filesystem; it names no curve, concrete, form, scope, count, identity, or context value; the count arrives in its params and the typed contexts and the admitted identity in its payload

  * `[ ]`   `role`
    * `[ ]`   App module in the adapter role: a function the harness's vectors own, beneath the `vectors` module of `apps/harness-crypto`
    * `[ ]`   Owns `MintMutationField`, `TransferMutationField`, `MINT_MUTATION_FIELDS`, `TRANSFER_MUTATION_FIELDS`, `MintMutation`, `TransferMutation`, `StatementMutationsVector`, and the function's deps, params, payload, success, error, return, and signature types; consumes `VectorCount`, which `harness-crypto/generate/evm/group_vectors` owns, `DeliveryContextField`, which `harness-crypto/generate/evm/challenge_vectors` owns, and `AdmittedKemIdentity`, `mint`, `MintErrorReturn`, `transfer`, and `TransferErrorReturn`, which `harness-crypto/delivery/mint` and `harness-crypto/delivery/transfer` own
    * `[ ]`   Adds its module line to `apps/harness-crypto/src/vectors/mod.rs` and its re-export line to `apps/harness-crypto/src/vectors/provides.rs`, both of which `harness-crypto/vectors/cross_holder` creates, so the function is a public entry of the crate through the crate barrel; the composing function and the sets' success and error types are `harness-crypto/vectors`'s
    * `[ ]`   Obtains every baseline statement and proof from the delivery functions and composes no delivery step; verifies and reads and rebuilds proof components through the delivery-proof adapter it is handed; does not compute a challenge, a response, a first message, or a pairing product
    * `[ ]`   Does not choose a count, a scope, a purpose, an identity, or a context value, and does not admit a context or an identity; the configuration carries them, `harness-crypto/config/admit_statements` admits the identity, `harness-crypto/run/kem_consumer` pairs it with its setup scope as an `AdmittedKemIdentity`, `harness-crypto/run/admit_contexts` admits each context into the forms, and the caller passes them here
    * `[ ]`   Does not construct a pairing, KEM, key agreement, delivery proof, randomness source, or forms; the caller obtains each through its factory or `chain/create_chain_forms` and hands this function the borrowed adapters
    * `[ ]`   Does not encode, report, write, or submit a record
    * `[ ]`   Does not carry a commit
